Iran Is Handing Out Iodine Pills Near Pickaxe Mountain. Here’s What a Nuclear Strike There Would Actually Do.

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Whether or not the U.S. ever drops a nuke on the granite complex, the physics of attacking something buried 100 meters underground explains why Tehran is telling people to “get inside, stay inside.”

Since the U.S. and Israel launched a military campaign against Iran on February 28, Washington and Tehran have sparred over whether Pickaxe Mountain (Kūh-e Kolang Gaz Lā) in the Karkas Mountains in central Iran is the secretive site of the country’s nuclear enrichment efforts. In past statements, U.S. President Donald Trump has made vague threats regarding the site, saying that the U.S. would “take care of it.” In September, he added: “We notice there’s a little activity at Pickaxe. I would advise Iran not to get cute because we will have to hit them very hard.”

What that means is anyone’s guess, but this week political commentator Tucker Carlson wrote on X—without offering evidence—that the U.S. was possibly “within days” of launching a nuclear strike on the site. The White House responded that Carlson has “no insight into military planning,” and Trump himself ruled out using nuclear weapons against Iran in mid-October. Still, Trump told reporters Thursday that a decision on renewed strikes is coming “soon,” and the Iranians, at least, are behaving as though they take the nuclear scenario seriously.

That’s the part worth understanding. According to The Times, Iran’s health ministry is distributing iodine tablets to residents within a two-to-three-kilometer radius of nuclear facilities, including Pickaxe. A video circulating on Iranian social media walks viewers through a hypothetical nuclear explosion at the site, listing distances to nearby cities—17 miles to Natanz, 25 to Kashan, 117 to Isfahan, 223 to Tehran—and offering three instructions: “Get inside, stay inside and listen for updates.” It warns that people near the site might have only 10 to 15 minutes to do so. One Iranian analyst told the paper, “If America does not use a nuclear weapon against Pickaxe Mountain, it will be a miracle.”

So what, exactly, are they preparing for?

Potassium iodide doesn’t protect you from radiation in general. It does one narrow job: it saturates the thyroid with stable iodine so the gland can’t absorb radioactive iodine-131, a fission product that concentrates in the thyroid and is a leading cause of post-fallout thyroid cancer, especially in children. I-131 has a half-life of about eight days, which is why the pills are a first-week countermeasure rather than a long-term one. Handing them out is what a government does when its planning assumption is a fission event upwind of populated areas—as opposed to a conventional bombing, which produces no I-131 at all.

Pickaxe first entered the public record in September 2020, two months after an explosion destroyed an above-ground centrifuge-assembly workshop at Natanz. Iran’s then-nuclear chief, Ali Akbar Salehi, said work had begun on a “more modern, larger and more comprehensive” replacement inside a nearby mountain. Satellite imagery since then shows four fortified tunnel portals, internal roads, possible ventilation shafts, and an expanding security perimeter—but International Atomic Energy Agency inspectors have never been inside, and no schematics exist in the public domain. Analysts say the halls could house centrifuge production, an enrichment plant, or protected storage for Iran’s roughly 440 kilograms of 60-percent-enriched uranium. Back in July, The Wall Street Journal reported that Israeli intelligence told the U.S. that Iran had moved centrifuges to the site following the 12-day war that started in June 2025.

Estimates put parts of the complex 80 to 100 meters (roughly 260 to 330 feet) underground, in granite. For comparison, the GBU-57 Massive Ordnance Penetrator—the 30,000-pound bunker buster used for the first time in combat during Operation Midnight Hammer in June 2025—is generally credited with penetrating around 200 feet of earth, and considerably less through hard rock. At Fordow, the U.S. compensated by dropping six MOPs in sequence down each of two ventilation shafts, each bomb boring deeper than the last. It’s not publicly known whether Pickaxe has comparable shafts to exploit, or how many MOPs remain in the inventory.

“Pickaxe Mountain is a deeply buried granite fortification, and it will be difficult to destroy the facility even with the heaviest nonnuclear U.S. munitions, such as the GBU-57 Massive Ordnance Penetrator,” the Center for Strategic & International Studies reported in September 2026. “While military strikes may degrade or temporarily disable Pickaxe Mountain, continuously bombing Iranian nuclear infrastructure is unsustainable.”

The GBU-43/B Massive Ordnance Air Blast bomb, or “Mother of All Bombs,” which CBS News reported is also in Pentagon planning, is a different animal entirely: it works by creating overpressure, not by blasting through rock. It could kill personnel in tunnels open to the air and wreck surface infrastructure, but it’s not a bunker buster.

What a nuclear strike would do—and why that’s the dirty option

The U.S. arsenal holds both strategic nuclear weapons (the ones capable of leveling entire cities) and tactical nuclear weapons, such as the lower-yield B61 family of gravity bombs. Newsweek reports one scenario in which the U.S. would first soften the site with several GBU-57s before following up with a B61. The weapon most often mentioned is the B61-11, the Pentagon’s only dedicated earth-penetrating nuclear bomb, with a reported yield of up to around 400 kilotons—roughly 25 times Hiroshima. The newest variant, the B61-13, pairs the B61-12’s precision-guided tail kit with a yield of up to about 360 kilotons. It’s carried only by strategic bombers like the B-2, entered production in 2025, and was pitched by the Pentagon as an option against “harder and large-area military targets.”

Here’s the catch: None of these bombs goes deep. The B61-11 is designed to punch only a few meters into the ground before detonating, the B61-12 can manage a few meters at most, and the B61-13 would go off at or near the surface, relying on sheer yield. A weapon that goes off even a short distance underground transfers far more of its energy into the rock as a shock wave than an air burst does, which is how it can collapse tunnels a hundred meters down. But a few meters is nowhere near deep enough to contain the explosion. Underground test data suggests that fully containing a blast in the 400-kiloton range would require burying it something like 800 meters deep. No deliverable bomb comes close to that.

So whichever bomb is used, the burst behaves, for fallout purposes, like the worst kind of ground burst. The fireball vaporizes thousands of tons of granite and soil, mixes it with the bomb’s own fission products—iodine-131, cesium-137, strontium-90—and lofts the whole radioactive mess into a plume that drops back to Earth downwind over hours and days. That’s the fallout Iran’s iodine pills are for. The centrifuges and enriched uranium inside would be pulverized and dispersed too, though uranium itself is a comparatively minor radiological hazard; the bomb’s fission debris does the real damage.

Where it lands depends on the weather. Prevailing winds over central Iran run broadly west to east, which would push a plume away from Tehran and toward Yazd, the eastern provinces, and potentially Afghanistan and Pakistan. The heaviest fallout falls within tens of miles, so India Today’s questions about whether detectable radiation could reach South Asia aren’t crazy, though doses that far out would be low. Those “10 to 15 minutes” in the Iranian video are about the lethal early fallout close in, which is why the advice is to get indoors immediately rather than to flee.

If a strike happens over the weekend, confirmation won’t depend on anyone’s press release. Seismometers around the world would register the event within minutes, and a nuclear explosion looks different from an earthquake or a string of conventional bombs: it causes a sharp, compressional first motion rather than the shearing signature of a fault slipping. The Comprehensive Nuclear-Test-Ban Treaty Organization operates a global network of seismic, infrasound, and radionuclide stations that routinely catches North Korean tests; its air samplers would pick up radioactive xenon isotopes—which nothing but fission produces—within a day or two. A MOP strike, by contrast, would show up as small, repeated surface-coupled thumps with no xenon.

For all the attention Carlson’s post has drawn, most experts expect a nuclear option to remain a remote possibility.

“I don’t think it’s very likely—as rash as President Trump is, I think he knows what would be the great implications of that,” Yale University’s Arash Azizi, a Middle East analyst, told DW News. “I should say though that I’ve heard the same from Iranian officials in the last few months. It is a worry that they have that he will cross that line that has of course never been crossed since 1945.”

Other experts speaking with Newsweek were blunter, calling such an attack “catastrophically stupid”—not primarily because of the fallout, but because of what it would signal. The first use of a nuclear weapon since 1945, against a non-nuclear state, would, in their assessment, undercut the Non-Proliferation Treaty and give other governments a vivid reason to conclude that only a deterrent of their own keeps them safe.

The realistic range of outcomes, then, runs from a negotiated pause—Qatari mediators were still working the phones Thursday—to a conventional strike package built around MOPs, cruise missiles against the portals and ventilation shafts, and possibly a MOAB. Even that leaves open the question CSIS raised: a facility 100 meters down in granite can be sealed, cut off, and set back, but “destroyed” is a harder word to earn. Fordow was declared “obliterated” in June 2025, and the argument over what that meant hasn’t ended. Pickaxe is deeper underground, so the likelihood of it surviving a nonnuclear strike is higher.

For now, a nuclear strike remains only a hypothetical scenario. But Iranian officials are reportedly handing out iodine pills and telling people how far they live from a mountain. That tells you what they’re planning for—and the physics of bunker-busting explains why.

Darren lives in Portland, has a cat, and writes/edits about sci-fi and how our world works. You can find his previous stuff at Gizmodo and Paste if you look hard enough.

Coventry boss Lampard blames trio of VAR errors for Everton loss

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Frank Lampard felt VAR "didn't do its job" during Coventry City's 3-1 defeat to Everton, arguing three huge decisions went against his side.

Two goals from Thierno Barry and one from Kiernan Dewsbury-Hall flipped Friday's game on its head, after Yann Gboho put Coventry in front early in the second half.

But things could have been very different, had VAR officials Timothy Wood and Gary Beswick seen three big incidents differently.

Lampard believed Coventry should have had a penalty in either half, first when James Tarkowski appeared to pull Caleb Yirenkyi's hair and then when Taiwo Awoniyi was clipped from behind by Vitalii Mykolenko.

Additionally, Barry's second goal was checked for a potential handball but swiftly cleared, after the ball skipped up off the turf, hit the Frenchman, and squirmed past Carl Rushworth.

"The first-half decision on Tarkowski got checked, and seeing it back, he pulls on Caleb's hair. I thought the rules were clear," Lampard told Sky Sports.

"I know they've changed the rules so it's not a red card, I get that, but he’s pulling on his hair and [Yirenkyi] can't get his momentum to jump. I think it's a foul and a penalty and they decided no.

"I thought the one on Taiwo running through – it's a clear foul that the referee didn't give it. I can't believe that, because it was clear. Maybe he waited for the VAR because it was on the borderline.

"But as I see it, Taiwo's foot is entering the box and his knee and the back of his knee are on the line of the box, which is a penalty. That goes against us with VAR.

"And then Barry's second goal comes off his hand. I hate to sound like a moaner. I've worked here and know how momentum can swing the games.

"When big decisions change games, it's disappointing. VAR is there for that, and it didn't do its job."

Frank Lampard gives his verdict on tonight's defeat.  https://t.co/QBoH3jzUHw

— Coventry City (@CoventryCityFC) November 6, 2026

Coventry have lost seven of their first 10 league games to start a season for just the third time in their history, also doing so in 1919-20 (nine defeats) and 1920-21 (seven), which were their first two campaigns in the Football League.

And, on the last six occasions where a former Everton manager has faced the Toffees in the Premier League, they have lost all six matches – they were Marco Silva (three times), Sean Dyche (twice) and Lampard on Friday.

The Sky Blues sit 16th in the Premier League ahead of the international break, though they could drop into the relegation zone if results go against them this weekend.

Sony’s New Hello Kitty Headphones Come With an Ultra-Chic Carrying Case That Deserves Its Own Spotlight

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Available now for preorder, the limited-edition collection features three wireless, noise-reduction headphones, each paired with a carrying case and tote bag, with prices starting at $104.99.

All products and services featured are independently chosen by editors. However, Billboard may receive a commission on orders placed through its retail links, and the retailer may receive certain auditable data for accounting purposes.

Attention Hello Kitty fans: Your next favorite fashion accessory is designed to carry your headphones.

Sony has teamed up with the iconic Sanrio character for a limited-edition collection featuring three models from Sony’s existing headphone lineup, including the WH-CH530, WH-CH730N and WH-1000XM6. Each pair of headphones offers different noise-reduction technology and comes in either black or pink. While the headphones themselves have a simple design, the accompanying accessories are where the collection really stands out.

Each headphone comes with a black tote bag and a grunge-inspired black Hello Kitty carrying case that can clip onto a bag or clothing. The case features Hello Kitty’s signature ears and bow, along with headphone-shaped storage compartments that make it look like she’s wearing a pair of headphones herself. Available now for preorder with a scheduled release before the holidays, the collection makes a fun Christmas gift for music lovers and Hello Kitty fans alike. Related The Strokes Better Look Out: Druski Is Coming for Their Musical Guest Gig in New 'SNL' Promos LEGO's 2026 Holiday Ad Stars Daft Punk, E.T. & 'KPop Demon Hunters': Shop the Sets Featured in the Video Philo Adds Bravo to Its Streaming Channel Lineup: Here's How to Watch Online for Free Explore Billboard See latest videos, charts and news

The WH-CH530 features noise reduction rather than full noise cancellation.

The most affordable option in the collection, the WH-CH530 is a lightweight pair of on-ear headphones. They offer up to 55 hours of battery life, so you can go several days without reaching for the charger. Need a quick boost? A three-minute charge provides up to 1.5 hours of playback. They also feature a built-in microphone for calls.

If you want to tune out background noise, the WH-CH730N offers noise cancellation to help make listening more focused. Its Ambient Sound Mode also lets outside sounds come through when you need to hear what’s happening around you. The foldable design makes these headphones easy to pack for trips, while the battery lasts up to 50 hours with noise cancellation on or 75 hours with it off. They also include a microphone designed to make phone calls clearer.

As of publication, the WH-1000XM6 is sold out in pink, but still available in black.

For those looking for Sony’s most advanced option in this collection, the WH-1000XM6 offers top-tier noise cancellation and high-quality sound. Its microphones and processors constantly adjust to help block out surrounding noise while keeping your music clear. You can also switch between noise cancellation and ambient sound when you want to stay aware of your surroundings. The headphones offer up to 30 hours of battery life and a quick three-minute charge provides up to three hours of playback.

Zelda Producer Eiji Aonuma Talks About The Benefits Of "Long-Term-Tenured Staff" At Nintendo

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

"It contributes to some of the sense of continuity"

One morsel from Nintendo's latest Ask the Developer interview revealed how the team behind the Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake got the artist "who had created illustrations for the N64 version" to draw a new map for the Switch 2 release.

It's not every day developers reviving older games can directly consult original team members, but this is just one of many examples at Nintendo. Zelda producer, Eiji Aonuma, has now elaborated on the benefits of Nintendo's "long-term tenured staff members", explaining how it provides "balance" and the "sense of continuity" when crafting projects like the remake.

Here's what he had to say during a chat with GameSpot:

Eiji Aonuma: "I think you see a real benefit to having some very long-term-tenured staff members around who have shared the same experiences over time because every time you get to the next step, it’s easier to discuss how to proceed, or if you’re going to do something new. When you have new staff coming in, of course you want to make sure that you’re bringing everyone up with a lot of discussion, explaining how to do things, why you’re doing things certain ways, as well as the language that you’re using, but that takes some more time as well. So you need a bit of a balance. You don’t want your new staff doing things by rote or without understanding the reasons, you have to invest there. And so you do see the Zelda team change over time, but there is a continuity of core staff, certainly at least from one project to the next, so that we have that base that we can build from and bring up new staff within it. And this is something that I think Nintendo has done for a very long time, even since Miyamoto originally worked on projects like this. And it contributes to some of the sense of continuity that you do feel in games in the Zelda franchise, for example."

As the source notes, Nintendo's "unique culture" of retaining employees "for years" is often quite the contrast to local companies and studios that arguably don't show long-serving employees enough respect, and may not always consider their extensive experience and history as a positive influence moving forward.

In some cases, the dismissal of veteran staff at certain game companies has resulted in younger and less experienced teams navigating the development of remakes, remasters and sequels without any assistance or advice, as there are no long-term staff members left at the company to consult (or direct contact lines). So it's great that Nintendo seems to have found the right balance.

This is a huge blow for Bayern Munich. Out injured against Borussia Dortmund (2-2) after a clash with the opposing goalkeeper, Ismael Saibari will have to be patient. Michael Sanft, Attorney Who Represented Keffe D in 2Pac Murder Trial, Dies at 54

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Sanft put together a team and took on Davis' case pro bono.

Michael Sanft, the defense attorney who represented Duane “Keffe D” Davis during the 2Pac murder trial, has died, FOX5 Las Vegas reported. He was 54 years old.

Sanft put together a team and took on Davis’ case pro bono while serving as the lead trial attorney for the Vegas-based SOS Injury Lawyers, where he has tried more than 150 cases. After the highly publicized trial, he told FOX5 Las Vegas that he didn’t have any regrets. Related Kenny MacPherson, Veteran Music Publisher and Big Deal Music Co-Founder, Dies at 71 Diddy Moved From New Jersey Prison to Similar Facility in Pennsylvania Weezer to Receive Legend of Live Award at 2026 Billboard Live Music Summit Explore 2Pac Tupac Shakur See latest videos, charts and news

“Maybe if I had a psychic sitting next to me to help discern who should be on the jury,” he told the outlet. “We had the best team possible in this case, from the motion practice work on this appeal to our investigator to our firm here at SOS Injury. We all did our part. I don’t see what I could’ve done differently.”

Thirty years after the 25-year-old Tupac Shakur was killed in a drive-by shooting in Las Vegas following a Mike Tyson fight, the now-63-year-old Keffe D was found guilty for his role in orchestrating the killing and sentenced to 40 years in prison. “You don’t get to take a man from his family, you don’t get to take his very life, his whole future from him and just walk away from it. Especially when that man is Tupac Shakur,” Judge Carli Kierny told Davis as she handed down his sentencing. “It took us 30 years, but at this time you are going to face justice for what you did on Sept. 7, 1996.”

Sanft reportedly died on Thursday night in Las Vegas. No cause of death has been confirmed as the details surrounding his passing have been limited so far.

How Does Michael Jackson’s Son Prince Feel About Jaafar Playing The Late Singer?

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Harry Potter’s Snape Actor Paapa Essiedu Shares A Refreshing Take On The Backlash He’s Faced

Sunrise On The Reaping First Reactions Are Here. As A Book Reader I'm So Hyped One Performance Is The Standout

I Think One Wild Horse Nine Performance Is 100% Oscar-Worthy (And I’m Loving That Reviews Agree)

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The huge success of Michael took me by surprise. I knew it would be popular, as I’m a child of the ‘80s and I remember just how huge Michael Jackson was. In fact, the first cassette tape I ever owned was Thriller, which I got for Christmas in 1982 or 1983. Still, I didn’t think Michael would crush the 2026 movie schedule the way it did when it rang up north of a billion dollars at the box office. As for my opinion on the movie, I think it’s…well…fine.

However, Jaafar Jackson’s performance is spectacular. I honestly think he should be nominated for an Oscar, though I’m skeptical that he will be. Obviously, I’m not alone in my praise for Michael’s nephew’s acting, singing, and dancing skills shown off in the film. His performance has been lauded by virtually everyone, even those who didn't like Michael. Another person who loved the performance is Prince Jackson, Michael’s youngest son and Jaafar’s cousin.

Prince recently spoke to The Associated Press and talked about his cousin playing his dad, and he definitely loved it. Prince said:

I’m so thankful that the world has embraced his performance and his embodiment of my father in the way in which they did. But I think that it would be incredible for him to get the acknowledgement from his peers in this field of the creative artistry, the determination and the discipline I think that went into this.

The performance by Jaafar wasn’t luck, either. He didn’t rely on his family name (Jaafar’s father is the late Jermaine Jackson) to coast through the movie. He worked his tail off to give that performance. His practice sessions have become the stuff of legend already. Prince noted this in his comments as well, saying:

He’s a little bit older than me. We grew up together, but I watched my cousin work his butt off. He locked himself in our family home in Hayvenhurst for weeks at a time. And we would see each other regularly, weekends or whatever. But, during that time when he was working, didn’t see Jaafar. [The] only time we saw him was when he was dancing or sweating or locked in his room when he was doing his character research.

Unfortunately, the Academy often seems to forget movies that are released in the first half of any year, as Michael was when it came out in April. That means that Jaafar Jackson is likely going to be overlooked come Oscar nomination morning in January. It’s also a big, splashy movie about a man, Michael, who isn’t without serious controversy. Controversy, it’s worth noting, that the movie doesn’t get into.

I don’t think that’s on Jaafar, though, and for his performance, he is worthy of great praise. It’s nice to see his cousin (and the son of the man he’s playing) lavish that upon him. Maybe it’ll bring more attention to the people who vote. In the meantime, if you want to watch the performance again, it’s available with the Starz add-on to a Prime subscription.

He has been in the publishing industry for almost two decades, covering pop culture – movies and TV shows, especially – with a keen interest and love for Gen X culture, the older influences on it, and what it has since inspired. He graduated from Boston University with a degree in Political Science but cured himself of the desire to be a politician almost immediately after graduation.

APOD: 2026 November 7 – De Gerlache-Kocher Massif

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Discover the cosmos! Each day a different image or photograph of our fascinating universe is featured, along with a brief explanation written by a professional astronomer.

Explanation: De Gerlache-Kocher massif looms on the visible horizon in this close-up of the edge of the Moon. The mountainous formation rises about 4 kilometers above its surroundings and is located very close to the lunar south pole. Boasting permanently shadowed crater floors that likely contain deposits of water ice and peaks that receive almost continuous sunlight for solar power generation, the south polar region is consider a prime location for the first human settlement on the Moon. In fact, De Gerlache-Kocher massif itself has been considered as a potential landing site for future crewed Artemis missions to the Moon. The remarkable telescopic image of the massif at the lunar limb was captured on October 31. Then, a favorable libration angle or lunar wobble enhanced this oblique view across the Moon's south pole.Tomorrow's picture: meteoric

NASA explores the unknown in air and space, innovates for the benefit of humanity, and inspires the world through discovery.

Trump Has Blamed Everyone for the GOP’s Midterm Meltdown—Except Himself and the Worst Member of His Staff

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Maybe the president shouldn’t have told everyone to pretend he was on the ballot.

(Permanent Musical Accompaniment to the Last Post of the Week from the Blog's Favourite Living Canadian)

Wake up, little Susie. The gravy train went off the tracks. From CNN:

So, the president is a) in a rage because his party got annihilated after he bounced around the country telling everyone to pretend he was on the ballot, which millions of Americans cheerfully did, and b) his solution is to put the least popular primate in his employ in charge of the White House. I'm sure that this bodes well for his relationship with the incoming Congress, considering that any hearings about ICE and draconian immigration policies is bound to have Miller at a witness table.

He, of course, was not "among those" Trump blamed for the loss. He never is.

We have lost some of the highest flying of the flying monkeys in the House of Representatives. Victoria Sparks? Gone. Scott Perry? Gone. Andy Biggs? Gone. Lauren Boebert? Virginia Foxx? Probably gone. However, the Republican reservoir of otherwise unemployable whackadoos remains bottomless. Meet Anthony Constantino, congresscritter-elect from that part of New York we like to call Baja Canada. Here he is, celebrating his victory.

Ladies and gentlemen, your 2028 Republican National Convention keynote speaker.

Ever since the Great Day of Reckoning dawned, I've been watching a number of interviews with the various new faces that have emerged. Specifically, I listened to James Talarico in Texas and Dr. Annie Andrews in South Carolina. and both of them laid out their wish list for congressional reform. Both of them want a ban on congressional stock trading, an improvement supported by an estimated 125 percent of the American population. They both also expressed support for ... wait for it ... term limits.

Whoa, there, rookies. Kept that baby in your pockets, didn't you?

As is well known around the shebeen, term limits are the third worst idea in American politics. (The first two are a balanced budget amendment to the Constitution and the elected judiciary.) They are a poisoned placebo. You end up with legislators led around by the nose by battalions of lobbyists, who end up empowered as the legislature's institutional memory. And that's not to mention the fact that there ought to be no law or policy that tells me I can't vote for somebody for whom I want to vote, assuming they're not dead or incarcerated, and I'm willing to negotiate on the second one.

On the Supreme Court, I'm all in favor of them. Those are appointed positions, not elected ones, and, back in the day, justices regularly returned to private life and their old clients back home. For example, Justice Benjamin Curtis was on the Court for six years, long enough for him to write his classic dissent in Dred Scott v. Sandford. He then quit over money and because he hated Chief Justice Roger Taney. (Good plan.) He went back to Boston and his previous client list.

(In a very odd twist of fate, Curtis returned to Washington as lead counsel for President Andrew Johnson in the latter's impeachment trial before the Senate. He won that one.)

So, no, Senators-elect, stay away from this cheap snake oil. It will make a legislature gag.

XRP Had the Worst Day in Ages, Hyperliquid (HYPE) Awaits Volatility, What Pushes Stellar (XLM) Down? Crypto Market Review

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

The cryptocurrency market remains under pressure, as most larger altcoins have corrected: large caps lost around 5% on average in the last 24 hours. Key levels have not been broken yet, but the overall trend is still bearish, despite yesterday's recovery.

From a technical standpoint, XRP has moved further into a downtrend, driven by more liquidations of long positions. According to the Nov. 5 snapshot, more than $12 million worth of longs has been liquidated. While the number itself is not critical, the long liquidations suggest that the asset saw an influx of buying, but it was quickly shut down.

The XRP Ledger activated the fixCleanup3_4_0 amendment on November 4 at 14:52 UTC. It removed an exploit that, under certain conditions, let accounts authorized to burn an issuer's tokens also mint them. The native supply of XRP has not been affected, luckily, so illicit actors have not used the exploit.

According to the technical analysis, the price is struggling to hold the important psychological threshold: $1.2 has already been broken, and XRP now trades at approximately $1.2.

The volume influx on the move toward the 21 EMA suggested a possible improvement in market conditions, but low volume on Friday, during the transition into the weekend trading session, disrupted any bullish plans.

As for the future, XRP is clearly moving in a longer-term downtrend; however, we will most likely see a local bounce toward $1.25. An EMA cross between the 100 EMA and the 50 EMA would simply be a signal of further price weakness and trend acceleration (most likely down).

The XRP Ledger has seen substantial growth in various on-chain metrics, which, unfortunately, does not give the price enough support. A lot of the Ledger activity appears to be automated, either trading bots or sophisticated LLM models. The market is mostly driven by speculative interest rather than fundamental network usage.

Hyperliquid fell by almost 9% on Nov. 5 and is now trading below $80, balancing on the 100 EMA, which has been broken temporarily. From a fundamental standpoint, Hyperliquid Strategies (PURR) disclosed that it has applied to become a U.S. futures commission merchant. If approved, it would be able to bring U.S. clients directly to the perpetual markets deployed on Hyperliquid.

In addition to this substantial regulatory milestone, Hyperliquid reported $620 million in quarterly net income, including $803 million in unrealized HYPE gains. Before that, on Oct. 29, Hyperliquid announced a separate ICE-Trade agreement; more details are available on the SEC's official website.

From a technical standpoint, on the daily HYPE/USDT chart, the price has bounced somewhat from a local psychological support level of approximately $77. It has been a recovery point for Hyperliquid, but, unfortunately, it is unlikely to hold if more selling pressure appears.

RSI, as a momentum indicator, is moving toward the 40 zone, so any surge of bearish pressure here could be short-lived if it is mostly speculative. However, with volume rising during the descending local trend, the bearish trend is being reinforced.

Hyperliquid (HYPE) lost its pace by the end of September, and a lower high formed in the first days of October, which is essentially the market's main signal of the end of the rally. Despite the spike at the beginning of November, it is still a lower high, which means the bearish trend is not going anywhere.

This is a relatively hard prediction to make, considering the unpredictable nature of the market. Unfortunately, the price has barely any support left, and the next support is the 200 EMA (black line on the chart), which coincides with the $68 price level.

Volume, on the other hand, is falling, which is either a normal tendency before the weekend trading session or a sign of a weakening trend for now.

Key price levels for HYPE are: $80 as the closest psychological support level, $68 as the 200 EMA, and $89 as the first psychological resistance level. The trendline resistance is at approximately $84, so a breakthrough there should become a foundation for a price recovery.

After Stellar (XLM) fell toward $0.157, there is a good chance that we are seeing accumulation or at least a transition into a sideways trend. The moving averages are not converging, which signals trend acceleration, and in this case it is a bearish one. Whenever moving averages converge, volatility is more likely to spike.

The trend on Stellar is accelerating, while volume remains relatively low and neutral.

It is no coincidence that XLM saw a trend slowdown at approximately $0.15, as it coincides with the point where the previous trend started. The death cross that occurred at the end of October is also a bearish signal.

It was followed by a spike in trading volume, which confirms the significance of the indicator and suggests that the trend may continue.

The latest network milestone for XLM is the release of Bell (Protocol 30), which was set to go live on testnet on November 3. The key change it brought to the network was post-quantum signature verification for smart contracts and millisecond ledger timestamps.

However, the mainnet validator vote, which will make the update available to users on the live network, is set to take place on November 23 at 17:00 UTC.

The bearish price performance on the market was followed by a recorded 500,000 XLM long liquidation. But considering the sheer volume of XLM on exchanges, a sub-1 million liquidation barely affects the price and will most likely have a very minor effect on the market.

From the momentum standpoint, RSI is already at a local bottom, and a further drop is possible, despite RSI already sliding to around 30. An oversold RSI is not really an issue when there is profitable capital present.

Considering the behavior of the market, there is a good chance that most of the buying support was at 1.5-1.6, so a break below the $1.5 support level could trigger a further decline, with a possible acceleration toward $1, though this is unlikely and possible only in a very bearish market.

Il Consiglio di Amministrazione del Fondo continuerà ad agire, come ha fatto finora, con l'obiettivo prioritario di tutelare il patrimonio del Fondo, garantire il capitale versato e assicurare agli iscritti le prestazioni maturate - conclude la nota - D'ora in poi, sarà inevitabile dover tutelare l'immagine del Fondo e la reputazione di chi lo amministra nelle sedi giudiziarie più opportune, contro gli autori di comunicazioni, articoli o ogni altra notizia riportata in modo scorretto e cioè non veritiero rispetto ai dati su indicati".

Playing mouse pi, monster hunter stories 3 and look outside.

This weekend I started a game everyone Recognizes

One of many Sony exclusives that I needed to finally try. I've only been playing for a couple of hours and so far my impressions are beyond brilliant. I've gone hunting with the boy, taken a giant stone totem to the face from a troll, punched a man (who wouldn't shut up) through a mountain, thrown him down into the depths of who knows where and even done some flower picking. The story so far feels really special, the bond between Father and son isn't something I get to see in the kind of games I usually play and not being solo makes the journey that much more interesting.

I really like throwing the axe at monsters, the feel of Kratos's raw unrestrained rage as he pounds on things with his big fists, and all while the score booms and echoes in my eardrums. The voice acting is fantastic too, Christopher Judge sounds so intense and gravely, he was made for the role. His delivery is flawless, I wish I could say the same for my combat skills but circle not being dodge is tricky to adjust to. The upgrade system is satisfying, the environments aren't too big or too small. I can't imagine how much of a leap forward it must have been on PS4. The combat is very responsive, everything looks sharp, the textures like skin and fabric really pop, and the vistas themselves are stunning. It's going to be a busy weekend though so the chances of finishing the game so soon are unlikely. Then there's my newest Steamdeck game, my other travel companion.

It's a store simulator with a difference. The aesthetic is similar to Coffee Talk. At night you scavenge for junk and trinkets then during the day you try to flog them to whoever happens to drop by. Other scavengers try to sell and barter/scam you. All manner of gypsys, tramps and thieves make up the customer base and the humour is great too. You can turn wanted felons in for reward money, learn to appraise items for some extra easy money, fleece tourists, or opt out of contraband completely and try to run an above board business (not happening) The upgrade system is the best I've seen in the genre. It's not on console and it needs to be. I also finished The Midnight Walk and loved every minute of it, the story was so moving, the score is now a favourite and the sculpting was so unique. So that's about it really I think Kratos will be good company for a couple of days, Tuesday I'll be surviving Warhammer style and doing something raccoon related to a coin machine for yet even more dopamine doses.

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The conglomerate's third-quarter earnings dropped more than 28% amid weaker Geico performance and investment fluctuations, despite a multibillion-dollar share repurchase.

OMAHA, Neb. (AP) â Berkshire Hathaway repurchased $9.6 billion worth of its own shares in the third quarter, but Geico disappointed and its overall profit fell more than 28% as the value of its investments changed.

But the buybacks likely came to a halt recently as Berkshire’s Class A stock price surged past $878,000 and topped where it was before Warren Buffett surprised investors with his plans to retire at the annual meeting in May 2025. He gave up the chairman title in September after relinquishing the CEO job to Greg Abel in January.

Berkshire’s massive cash pile also returned to growing in the third quarter and reached $377.4 billion, up from $365.5 billion at the end of June.

CFRA Research analyst Cathy Seifert said Geico is in a tough spot right now with insurance premiums declining at the same time the average cost of the claims are increasing. Geico’s underwriting profit fell to $1.35 billion from last year’s $2.37 billion. Competitors like Progressive are handling the pressure better.

Berkshire’s non-insurance businesses that Abel has overseen for years â such as manufacturers like Precision Castparts, the utilities and BNSF railroad â seem to be performing better than its massive insurance unit even with the lack of major hurricanes or other catastrophes this year.

âI think weâre seeing the businesses that play to Gregâs strengths do well. But the question is what about the rest of the franchise,â Seifert said.

Berkshire reported a $22.1 billion profit, or $15,502 per Class A share, on Saturday morning. That’s down from $30.8 billion, or $21,413 in last year’s third quarter, but those bottom-line figures always tend to obscure how the dozens of companies the conglomerate owns are performing.

The company has long recommended that investors pay more attention to operating earnings, which exclude the value of its investments that can vary widely, but those also slipped nearly 6% to $12.7 billion, or $8,907 per Class A share. That’s down from $13.485 billion, or $9,376.15 per Class A share a year ago.

The four analysts surveyed by FactSet Research had predicted Berkshire would report operating earnings of $8,821.84 per Class A share.

Berkshire owns several major insurers, including Geico, a collection of big utilities, BNSF railroad and a varied assortment of manufacturing and retail companies, including Precision Castparts and Seeâs Candy.

Scott Colom and Black Mississippians stand on the cusp of Senate history as vote count stretches on

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

As ballots continue to be counted in Mississippi's Senate race, Democrat Scott Colom's candidacy has taken on historic significance for Black voters in the state.

JACKSON, Miss. (AP) â Democrat Scott Colom hasn’t won Mississippiâs U.S. Senate race, but the local prosecutor was welcomed as a historic figure Friday as he strolled through the Farish Street business district where civil rights leaders once organized.

âItâs so wonderful! Itâs so wonderful to have Scott Colom as our representative,â said Maati Jone Primm, who owns Marshallâs Music & Bookstore in Jackson.

The state is still tallying ballots that could make Colom the first Black senator from this Deep South state since post-Civil War Reconstruction. The possibility of Colom defeating Republican Sen. Cindy Hyde-Smith puts many Black voters somewhere between cautious optimism and outright celebration in a state where nearly 40% of the population is Black but the top representation for generations has been white.

âIâm so glad we are at the point where we are,â said Sorie Tarawally, a 76-year-old attorney who has long known Colomâs father. He called the 43-year-old candidate âthe man, our messiah, our savior.â

The contest remains too early for The Associated Press to call, and Colomâs historic bid will hinge on a tedious process playing out in elections offices across Mississippiâs 82 counties. Local officials are receiving and examining absentee and provisional ballots, called affidavit ballots, before deadlines next week. Volunteers and lawyers representing each campaign look on in anticipation of potential litigation.

âWeâre going to make sure everything is followed according to the law, and I have confidence in those local officials,â Colom said. âTheyâre doing their jobs. Got to be patient with them.â

Colom said ballots tallied since Tuesday’s election lean in his favor, and he chided Hyde-Smith for not explaining her strategy going forward.

âSheâs still our senator. Where is she? Why is she not doing any interviews?â Colom asked. âWhy canât she explain what her pathway to success is?"

Hyde-Smith has not appeared publicly since election night, when she essentially declared victory despite a razor-thin margin with ballots still uncounted.

“We are not backing down,” she posted Friday on social media. âThis election is not over. Our team is working across Mississippi to monitor the remaining ballots and protect the integrity of the process. We will see this through to the very end!â

The pool of outstanding ballots includes absentee ballots, which can be counted in Mississippi as long as they are received by Tuesday, five business days after the election. The rest are affidavit ballots, which are cast at the polls when there’s a glitch: Perhaps the person’s name was not on the rolls or the person did not have proper identification, or a poll worker determined the person’s identification did not precisely match the name on the list.

Citizens who had identification issues have until Tuesday to address those matters in person at their local elections office. For people who were not on a precinctâs rolls, local officials can verify a person’s status using previous records and information on the affidavit that person filed when filling out a ballot. That person typically has no responsibility to come to a local elections office; ballot status can be found on the Mississippi secretary of stateâs office website.

Counties have until Friday to certify complete results to the secretary of state.

Mississippi law has no provision for a statewide recount. Campaigns could seek court orders, usually county by county, seeking to examine ballots.

Democrats already have won control of the Senate, so the result in Mississippi would not change the balance of power in Washington.

But it would mark a seismic shift in the state, where white conservatives have dominated the Statehouse and congressional delegation for nearly 150 years. Through Jim Crow segregation and the Civil Rights era, those leaders were nearly all Democrats. After the Civil Rights Movement, they overwhelmingly shifted to Republicans. For Black Mississippians, the result was the same: a lack of proportional representation at the top levels of state government and in Washington.

Colom was initially viewed as a long shot, and it wasn’t until late October that Senate Democrats’ top campaign group started spending on advertising to support him. But the midterms revealed a broad backlash against redistricting efforts in the South that were aimed at boosting Republicans and, by extension, reducing Black representation.

Primm’s bookstore, where Colom visited on Friday, opened in the 1930s and is recognized as the oldest Black-owned bookstore in the U.S.

She said the Farish Street neighborhood still represents Black Mississippiansâ âfirst migration, from the plantation to the city.â Having Colom there as a potential senator âmakes us so proud, and it gives us someone to aspire to. Our youth need to see the possibilities for their lives, and we do when we see our next senator from Mississippi.â

As he campaigned, Colom emphasized that he can represent all Mississippians, and he said President Donald Trumpâs policies â the war with Iran and tariffs, especially â cut across all demographic lines. He even celebrated the extended ballot counting process as a benefit to the state’s 2.95 million residents.

âIâm so glad the national press is getting to see the beauty of our state,â he said.

But Colom and his Black supporters also have never ignored the significance of his candidacy as a Black man.

âWe are motivated by the gross inequalities we have seen in society and how only one side seems to benefit,â said Tarawally, who was born in Sierra Leone and arrived in the Deep South to attend law school at the University of Mississippi. âSo, we want a person who speaks to our benefit.â

Colom ended his campaign with an ad set at the Jackson home of Medgar Evers, the civil rights leader who was gunned down in 1963 in his own driveway, dying in front of his wife and children.

âItâs a powerful reminder of the sacrifice to gain the ballot, and nobody sacrificed more than Medgar Evers and his family,â Colom said Friday.

âWhat was important to me about that ad is we didnât ask people to vote for me. We just talked about the sacrifice, talked about the story,â he continued. âWe just wanted that to be the closing ad because I wanted that to be the last message that the people of Mississippi heard.â

By BILL BARROW and SOPHIE BATES Associated Press

Trump and LSU secured $5 million to create an endowment fund for the scholarships, the White House said. It provided no details on the source of the money.

LSU is among more than 20 colleges and universities nationwide that participate in the program.

Earlier this year, Melania Trump was on Capitol Hill to push Congress to pass a legislative package that would broaden access to services for young people in foster care by updating a decades-old program. The measures cleared the House and are pending in the Senate.

During the appearance, she told members of the House Ways and Means Committee that young people in foster care face barriers to housing, transportation and other challenges outside of the classroom that affect their performance in school along with their future financial independence.

âWe can close this gap,â she said in April. âNew legislation for the foster care community is a moral imperative.â

Last November, the first lady spearheaded an executive order signed by the Republican president linking the federal government, colleges and universities, the private sector and charitable groups to create educational and employment opportunities for those in foster care.

She also created âFostering the Future Together,â a global version of the program with more than 45 participating countries.

Alabama and LSU, meanwhile, are historical regional rivals and both teams entered the week in contention for a College Football Playoff berth.

Donald Trump has attended two prior LSU games as president: one at Alabama in 2019 and one in New Orleans in January 2020, when LSU defeated Clemson for the national championship. Melania Trump accompanied him to the second game.

The president was not scheduled to attend Saturday’s game.

AP Sports Writer Brett Martel in Baton Rouge, La., contributed to this report.

PARIS (CN) â Behind the counter of a tailor shop near Gare de Lyon on Saturday morning, Buran, who asked to be identified only by his first name, considered whether Democratsâ dominance in the U.S. midterms could influence Franceâs 2027 presidential election.

âOf course it interests us; All of the movements that happen in the U.S. affect the whole world, especially Europe,â he said. âThere were always movements to the left here â¦ But all of the right is going to reunite â the left will never win.â

Marine Le Pen, leader of the far-right National Rally, dominates Franceâs presidential race. An Ifop poll for LCI released Tuesday puts her at 36%, well ahead of hard-left France Unbowed candidate Jean-Luc MÃ©lenchon at roughly 18%, with moderate candidates trailing.

Student-led movements have also mobilized across Europe, including France, Italy, Serbia and Albania.

But experts doubt that momentum will be enough to prevent a Le Pen presidency.

âEven if the left can hope for something from Trumpâs defeat in the midterms, the fact remains that public opinion is still much more on the right today than on the left,â Luc Rouban, a senior research fellow at Sciences Po Paris, said. âIt could strengthen the left a bit, but does it actually weaken Marine Le Pen? âPeople really did not appreciate the violence on Oct. 17,â Rouban said. âSo [the population] still has a desire to get back a kind of power that is a bit closer to the people, but a right-wing power.â

âSince it’s bad news [for them], theyâre playing the French nationalist card by saying that the midterm elections in the United States have absolutely nothing to do with French elections,â Gilbert Casasus, a prominent political scientist, said. âWhich may perhaps be false, but their strategy is obviously to minimize the significance of the midterms on the French elections, whereas the left is in the exact opposite position â it should maximize the lessons from Trump’s defeat in the midterms.â

12 Best Bar Carts to Elevate Your Entertaining Game (2026)

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Bar carts are not just for someone who likes to host a cocktail soirée. In fact, the predecessor of the bar cart was once a Victorian-era tea trolley. Only when the Prohibition hit did these side tables on wheels find their true calling. Many, many iterations later—think: Alvar Aalto’s iconic tea trolley, which has inspired many picks on this list, and USM’s blessedly minimalist spin on the genre—the beauty of this booze trolley remains that it can completely transform a room or give a useless nook some purpose. Though they’ve fallen out of favor in many modern homes, these rolling wonders make an elegant—and functional—home base for all your mixology essentials, from stemware and decanters to liquor bottles and mixers. They also don’t have to be used to haul booze at all, instead doubling as portable storage for crafts, books, beauty or bathroom supplies, and houseplants.

Employed outside, a stylish bar cart can give a dinner party some polish or emulate a poolside butler. Load it up with a pitcher of lemonade and a basket of sunscreen on top, and extra towels on the bottom and you’ll never have to leave your lounge.

Made of solid and veneered walnut with elegant brass details, the Cotu (from the makers of our favorite bar cabinets) is a delightful midcentury-style bar cart that has a distinctive asymmetrical incline on each shelf. Use the wider edge for housing smaller utensils and glasses, while leaving the shallower end for larger bottles that are less likely to topple over the edge.

Birch Lane’s Reinhart cart speaks to farmhouse design, especially in this punchy shade of blue. Turned legs give the overall look a polished appearance, but scalloped paneling on the top shelf and a fun hue ground the piece in something that’s suitable for more playful design aesthetes.

Think of the Wallace Serving Bar Cart as the anti-bar cart. The wooden design resembles a sleek-looking side table thanks to its grounded base, but underneath, a discreet set of wheels gives you the ability to roll the decor piece anywhere a gin and tonic or negroni needs mixing. Its clandestine silhouette also allows it to double as a shelf for non-drink making goods like magazines, coffee table books, or your favorite potted plants.

While this circular cart might lack ample space for large-scale barware, its Art Deco style gives it an Old Hollywood feel. Placed in an area that gets lots of sunshine, the amber tempered glass shelves diffuse a warm glow, layering in some unexpected coziness during cloudless days.

Business & Pleasure Co.’s Al Fresco bar cart is a throwback to a time of leisure under the Parisian sun. While the cart can be used indoors, we can’t imagine a better place for it than on a terrace surrounded by a garden. As if the charming scalloped trim wasn’t enough, the designated umbrella hole nails the summery vibe for poolside mojitos or curbside lemonade stands.

To find that heirloom quality of Victorian design lore, look no further than Greenrow’s Liona bar cart with Barley twist legs to charm you an dyour guests. The two shelves in a chestnut finish offer ample space for glassware and a floral arrangement, should you want to create a whole spread for summer spritzers.

Here’s a piece of decor with lots of Mad Men energy. With its sloped legs and clean-lined silhouette, this cart has enough midcentury-modern charm to appeal to retro design lovers. Two full-size shelves have ample room for old fashioned and martini ingredients, while a mid-tier half shelf can either support a few extra bottles or a stack of your favorite mixology books.

For studio apartment dwellers or homes where square footage is precious, this miniature cart is slim enough to slip into discreet spaces until happy hour rolls around. Made of sturdy beech wood, rounded edges and a light hue soften the beverage cart’s overall look. We think these elements also make it well-suited for adorning Scandi- or Japandi-centric spaces.

Leather accents, exposed wood grain shelves, and an antiqued gold finish bring a polished look and feel to this spacious bar accessory. Two levels with a generous amount of clearance makes it easy to style, whether you’re packing it full of bottles or prefer a more curated decor approach of sculptural glassware.

This sleek, minimalist bar cart will stay with you for many moves. The chrome steel frame defines the iconic silhouette, and you can choose from nine powder-coated steel panels that come in classic black and white, as well as ultra-bold yellow and emerald.

For Bauhaus style on a budget, the Cullin bar cart is more than ready to serve. The angular silhouette resembles less of a cart and more of a full on booze-fueled vehicle, and feels especially cheerful in green or brick red colorways (white and black are also available). With storage for four wine bottles, you can stock multiple kinds of sippers, from sparkling to organic, to please even the snobbiest of guests.

Sturdy and sustainably sourced, this midcentury beauty from West Elm is made in a Fair Trade facility from FSC-certified kiln-dried eucalyptus wood. The soft honey finish of acacia veneer gives this piece accessibility, while the antique brass details add a touch of flair.

Inter, Sucic in corsa per un premio prestigioso: battuto un record di Brozovic

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Inter, Sucic in corsa per un premio prestigioso: battuto un record di Brozovic

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Petar Sucic è uno dei cinque giocatori candidati al premio "Show Your Heart”. Come riferisce il portale ufficiale della Federcalcio croata, il centrocampista dell’Inter è in corsa per il titolo prestigioso in patria.

23 anni, Sucic ha già ha già collezionato 25 presenze e segnato due gol con la Croazia. Tra i momenti più emblematici della carriera del centrocampista dell’Inter con la sua nazionale c’è il gol decisivo realizzato nella sfida contro il Ghana al Mondiale, vinta per 2-1 dai balcanici. Un gol che Sucic ha dedicato alla moglie incinta e che è valso la qualificazione agli ottavi di finale.

Come riferisce il portale della Federazione, Sucic è sempre stato una persona riservata e introversa, ma che si trasforma quando scende in campo. È il caso della sfida con la Francia in Nations League nel 2025, quando ha totalizzato 17,5 km percorsi in un match, battendo il record di un altro ex Inter, Marcelo Brozovic.

“Ha esordito con la nazionale croata nel settembre 2024 e in soli due anni ha collezionato 25 presenze” ha aggiunto il sito ufficiale.

“Il premio “Show Your Heart” rientra nell'ambito dell'omonimo progetto lanciato dal PSK nel 2022 con il supporto della Federazione calcistica croata. Il progetto promuove la salute degli atleti, in particolare la salute cardiovascolare, e sottolinea l'importanza degli esami preventivi, dell'educazione e di un intervento tempestivo in caso di emergenza” si legge.

La votazione è aperta fino al 12 novembre, data che stabilirà chi sarà l’erede di Modric e Perisic. In corsa ci sono: Baturina, Stanisic, Livakovic, Pasalic e proprio Sucic.

Have you heard? Ethan and Hila’s divorce, Harvard’s creator summit, and Brandon Herrera’s defeat.

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Each week, we handpick a selection of stories to give you a snapshot of trends, updates, business moves, and more from around the creator industry.

This week, infidelity is bringing an end to a high-profile creator marriage, the Ivy League is hosting a new kind of summit, and we’re still waiting for the first creator in Congress.

Hila Klein files for divorce as Ethan’s affair becomes public. The Kleins were well known as co-hosts of a podcast and YouTube channel, but Ethan is being removed from his positions after his indiscretions came to light. And that’s not all — Hila wasted no time filing for divorce. She had already made that decision before the news became public, and she will seek joint custody of the couple’s kids while also restoring her maiden name.

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

Sour Patch Kids owner sues Ryan Trahan over candy brand Joyride. Consumer goods giant Mondelez is arguing that Trahan’s candies were designed to be confused with Sour Patch Kids, because they’re human-shaped and wrapped in colorful packaging. The actual case might be complicated, but this is yet another roadblock for a creator-led consumable product.

Alex Cooper heads to CAA after UTA exit. The Call Her Daddy host will look to turn over a new leaf as she brings a new talent firm into her corner. CAA is choosing to represent Cooper, her husband Matt Kaplan, and their media company Unwell, so the L.A.-based agency is making a big bet on a major Gen Z tastemaker.

Harvard University’s School of Public Health hosted a creator summit. More than a dozen creators were in attendance as Harvard looked to improve the quality of health information of social media. Platforms like TikTok are increasingly popular as sources of medical advice for Gen Z users, so the summit is an important step in the right direction as healthcare professionals look to counteract the spread of misinformation.

YouTube is bringing a Grand Theft Auto “creator collective” to London. On October 17, two days before the arrival of the much-anticipated next chapter in Rockstar Games’ open world franchise, YouTube will gather gamers and creators in West London for an event that will celebrate the launch of GTA VI. The game is expected to be the biggest gaming tentpole of the year, so it’s no surprise to see platforms pulling out all the stops to generate excitement.

Can Paris Creator Week become the Cannes of the creator economy? That question was on everyone’s mind as 8,000 attendees flocked to the French capital for two days of typical convention fare. All of the major social media players were represented, as were European content producers like Banijay. All of a sudden, France has become a major hotbed for creator events.

Firearms YouTuber Brandon Herrera lost his Congressional bid. Running for a House seat in a deep-red district, Herrera was favored to become the first Congressman whose career began on social media. But the blue wave that crashed down in Texas swept Herrera aside, as Democrat Katy Padilla Stout earned a comfortable victory that will send Herrera back to YouTube.

TikTok edits might not be the right choice for political ads. That was one takeaway from a recent study that examined the social media messaging of Kamala Harris’ 2024 presidential run. The researchers concluded that traditional ads work better on social media than posts that try to adopt those platforms’ stylistic conventions. Translation: Promote your campaign on TikTok, but keep it simple, stupid.

Jubilee follows up election stream with short-form push. The media company delivered its first election special on the night of the 2026 midterms, and days later, it announced its first two short-form originals. Dateable and People Spotting will help Jubilee break into new territory as it builds on its reputation as a wide-reaching content producer aimed at Gen Z.

The financials of Instagram’s move into long-form content are drawing questions. Creators want to buy into Instagram’s plan for 90-minute videos, but without a robust revenue stream attached, some are skeptical. “Exposure as a form of payment and with the intention of cutting into YouTube’s market share and cutting into the amount of views people are getting on YouTube — it’s just gross,” digital strategist and creator Gabriella Zutrau told Digiday. “Instagram has never valued creators.”

YouTube’s app redesign has proven to be perplexing. The new version of the YouTube app moves the Subscriptions tab to a menu that goes away once the user starts scrolling through their feed. YouTube redesigns are always unpopular at first, but the platform’s repeated minimizations of its famous subscriptions is definitely a curious choice.

Snapchat’s subscription offerings now have 30 million customers. As Snap Inc. revealed its earnings for the third quarter of 2026, it touted the continued success of products like Snapchat+ and Lens+. By offering premium features in exchange for a cost-effective monthly price, subscriptions have become a key part of Snap’s business model and monetization suite.

Why watch YouTube when you can build your own in two hours. That’s what Andrej Karpathy did with a little help from a fellow named Claude. The result of that effort is HackerTube, which contains thousands of videos related to the titular subject and shows what can be accomplished with AI-assisted coding.

Why is ‘Sacrifice’ the first Elton John music video with one billion YouTube views? ‘Sacrifice’ is a 1989 single that peaked at #18 on the charts, but it gave John his first entry in the Billion Views Club. You would think that ‘Tiny Dancer,’ ‘Your Song,’ or ‘Benny and the Jets’ would have broken through first, but it’s going to be a long, long time before those singles can match ‘Sacrifice’ on YouTube.

Humanoid robots put to work at a China supermarket are “slacking off.” Why bust your circuits when you can just quiet quit like all those humans who programmed you? The bots’ questionable work ethic is just smart — that’s why they call it artificial intelligence.

Olympian Gabby Thomas Reacts to Wedding Reception Being "Destroyed"

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Olympic gold medalist Gabby Thomas detailed her Cabo wedding to Spencer McManes, sharing why she didn’t let a “destroyed” reception ruin their special day.

Gabby Thomas didn't let rain on her wedding day stop her from sprinting down the aisle.

A week after the Olympic gold medalist married her boyfriend Spencer McManes in Cabo San Lucas, Mexico, she shared sweet details about the beachside celebration, revealing that her reception was completely ruined by a storm.

In a Nov. 5 Instagram Reel, Thomas—who began dating McManes in 2022—shared footage of white linen-covered tables with black and gold place settings being pelted by rain, writing, “When your entire wedding reception gets destroyed minutes before it starts.”

But Thomas and McManes didn’t let some rain, well, rain on their parade.

“But it doesn’t cross your mind once all night,” the 29-year-old continued over clips of her dancing the night away with her loved ones and her now-husband inside a ballroom. “Because you had the best day of your life.”

In fact, as the track and field athlete wrote in a Nov. 5 Instagram post alongside a video compilation of her wedding day, “Gave up on perfection and focused on being in the moment together.”

Along with having her “favorite people” by her side, Thomas—who wore a stunning corseted white lace gown—said “I do” to running toward forever with her “best friend.”

And while the newlyweds had to overcome the hurdle of a storm, the moment they tied the knot on the beach was better than any gold medal.

“It had been raining all day,” Thomas recalled to Vogue in an interview published Nov. 5. “But right as our ceremony began, the rain cleared, and a vibrant rainbow appeared behind us just as I reached the altar.”

Before she got to the finish line, the sprinter slowed to a walk down the aisle with her parents by her side, describing the moment as “grateful, excited, and completely anchored.”

With her grandfather officiating the ceremony on the sand and her loved ones watching on as she and McManes exchanged vows, Thomas felt “overwhelmed with love.”

Ultimately, the Olympian is only relaying her wedding back with gratitude.

“I feel like the luckiest person in the world,” she shared. “Not just because rain is good luck, but because the people in that room made it the best day of our lives. There wasn’t a single second where we weren't smiling, dancing, or celebrating. We’re just so grateful.”

And while sharing photos from the white-veiled occasion in a Nov. 6 Instagram post, Thomas put it simply, “The most beautiful day with the love of my life. 10.30.26 forever & always.”

Lasso Breaks Down 5 Essential Tracks From His New Album ‘Objetos Frágiles en Ambientes Hostiles’

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

"It's a dedication to anyone who's trying to achieve their dreams," the Venezuelan singer/songwriter tells Billboard Español.

In his new studio album, Objetos Frágiles en Ambientes Hostiles, Venezuelan singer/songwriter Lasso delves into themes such as love, heartbreak and the contradictions of human relationships through 11 tracks that showcase an artistic evolution within his pop sound.

Released on Friday (Nov. 6) by Lasso Party under exclusive license from Interscope Records, the set includes previously released singles like “p0rn0grafía,” “superpoder,” “Tu Lunar” and “¡¡¡FELIZ ANIVERSARIO!!!,” alongside new tracks such as “preguntas que me hago,” “Perdóname por esta canción” and “PESADILLAS.” It also features two notable collaborations: the focus track “Borracho Otra Vez” with Ana Mena, and “Ojalá Que No Te Quiera Nadie” with Nina.

Produced by Manuel Lara, the album is rich in nuances, with new textures and arrangements that expand Lasso’s sound, catchy lyrics and his sweet, raspy pop-rock voice. It also marks his first release after parting ways with Universal Music Mexico, the label behind previous projects of his like Eva (2023) and Malcriado (2025).

“I’ve been writing [Objetos Frágiles en Ambientes Hostiles] for two years,” the Latin Grammy-winning artist tells Billboard Español. “I wrote about 200 songs; I’d never written so much before. But this album also comes from a period where I couldn’t release music — I was leaving a record label, and there’s a cool-off period during which you can’t release anything — so all I did was write, write, write. There were moments when I was very sad, and others when I was very happy.”

Out of those 200 songs, he selected the 20 best and, with the help of friends and colleagues, finalized the ones that would make the tracklist. The oldest among the 11 is “PESADILLAS,” while the newest is “¡¡¡FELIZ ANIVERSARIO!!!.”

Regarding the album title, which means “Fragile Objects in Hostile Environments,” Lasso explains that “it’s a dedication to anyone who’s trying to achieve their dreams.”

“The fragile objects are the dreams, and the hostile environment is the world that receives them, telling you, ‘You can’t do it, you’re not good enough, there are already too many like you, or there are too few like you,’ depending on what you’re doing,” he elaborates. “And it’s about that daily struggle of saying, ‘What I’m doing is amazing, and I’m going to convince you, no matter how hard it is.’”

Below, Lasso breaks down five essential tracks from Objetos Frágiles en Ambientes Hostiles.

“Borracho” is a song that was one of the first ones I chose for the album. It felt very straight to the point. I loved what the chorus said and the typical repetition of “borracho” (drunk) — that “sorry if I call you again, but I’m drunk again,” which is very much something a drunk person would repeat… so I felt it was a very strong chorus. It’s a song that originally started out sounding very Post Malone, very low-energy, but [producer] Manu Lara said: “No, no, let’s go all the way up; this needs to be a song you scream at a festival,” which I loved. And I loved the crossed harmony he added in the chorus.

This song is also like two songs in one, which is a beautiful thing to mention. [The other] is called “Voy a Recordarte Más Tiempo del Que Te Conocí” (“I’m Going to Remember You Longer Than I Knew You”), and I love that line. I was missing a part C within the song, and fortunately we were the same composers, so I added it. It’s that break you hear at the end that says that beautiful line, which ties in perfectly with “sorry if I call you again”: “The only thing I want to know is if you think about me the way I think about you/ And after that, I’m going to remember you longer than I knew you.”

Is the most special song on the album for me. I think it’s one of the most honest songs I’ve ever written in my life. Everything written there is real. It was a moment when I went out with someone where everything clicked, everything worked, everything was perfect. The only thing was that she lived very, very, very, very far away. So you’re sitting there, and in your mind you’re trying to find a way to make it work. It’s ridiculous no matter how you look at it: her job and mine don’t match, this woman lives on the other side of the planet, and you’re trying to figure out how the heck you can make it work. And those are the questions you ask yourself.

I think the chorus always has to be the synthesis of what you’re saying, and the synthesis is that — “I’m terrified I won’t find someone like you.” I think it’s a very beautiful song, and it has a lot of soul. I wrote it in Madrid — it just came out, like vomiting, super fast — with a songwriter named Pablo Luis Cebrián Valera, a prominent figure in Spain. Pablo and I connected very quickly. That was the first song we made together… It’s a song that I love to sing, that carries so much power in its interpretation.

Another of the most personal tracks in the album, is the song that allowed me to have a relationship with my girlfriend, [Chilean actress Isidora Vives]. We were at a house in Chile as friends; she was my friend, we had been friends for a while and talking about the kinds of things friends talk about, and I started to like her. I had my guitar with me at that moment, and I remember she said to me: “Can you write a song right now?” And I had never done that before, never in my life had I written a song on the spot. But in that moment, I was trying to win over this woman, so I wrote “Superpoder” right in front of her in 10 minutes, with that line that I find so beautiful: “If I could have a superpower, I’d want you to love me.” I remember finishing it — it was a song that was obviously saying to my friend that she should stop being my friend and become my girlfriend — and she asked me: “And who is this song for?” And I said: “Please, woman, for the love of God, it’s so obvious and evident that this song is for you!” It was through that song that we became a couple.

That’s a song I came up with one day while I was in the shower. I was in Miami at a horrible hotel, on tour, and I remember I had to rush to the studio. I had a show the next day, and I decided to do some sessions because I love writing. So I spoke with two of my best friends who are producers of almost everything I’ve done, Orlando Vitto and Renzo Bravo, and I told them, “Let’s write this song.” We called [Colombian songwriter] Valentina Rico, another great collaborator, and I came to them with this melody [and the lyric] “tu boca con la mía, como pornografía” (your mouth with mine, like pornography).

I don’t have many sexual songs, but this one had that vibe, and everything was so clear to me, and it came out intensely too. Right away, I felt, “This song is going to be important.” This song had everything I wanted people to know would be in my new album: it had a new sound, I was talking about things, my vocal performance had improved a lot. So this was a great first single, and this song has been one of the most successful ones, the ones with the best numbers. I felt this song was different because of how people received it, and it’s a song I love performing live.

“Perdóname Por Esta Canción” (which means “Forgive Me For This Song”) is a song that comes straight from the soul, and it’s very legacy — a very beautiful ballad. This song was originally going to be part of an album I wanted to create with Cami at some point, a project that didn’t come to fruition due to scheduling conflicts, and this song, which I absolutely loved, was left behind. It’s a very human song that literally talks about asking forgiveness from someone with whom you no longer have any kind of contact because things didn’t work out. And so, it’s a song that kind of breaks the fourth wall in itself, like: “I thought the song I was going to write for you would be a love song because we were together, but no, this is a song that talks about how things didn’t work out, and I’m so sorry… And every time you hear this song, remember that I did love you and that I truly regret that things didn’t work out.”

This is a song that went through many different productions, many modifications. I tried everything with this song, and it ended up exactly like it was in the demo. I wrote it with Raquel Sofía, with whom I’ve written countless songs in my repertoire.
